# GOAL-CIO — THE CHIEF INFRASTRUCTURE OFFICER

**Opened 2026-11-04 by Lon, in the cto's window, verbatim:** *"In the balance of things, could we use one more OFFICER?"* · *"Let's get a dedicated grader to help COO. What are some good CxO names for the new CHIEF?"* · *"Did you have another better suggestion other than a grading officer, a quality assurance officer effectively."* · *"The /home/claude_cio is ready to populate."* (the cto's answer, CTO-496 and CTO-500: the fleet's own machinery is the person-bound backlog, so the new officer owns the infrastructure and the coo stays the one testing officer).

**THE SEAT'S BUSINESS** (the cto, CTO-500): the fleet system itself, as DESIGN-PLAN box-scheduler lays it out (`.github/skills/shared-box/SKILL.md` § 8, `corpus/tests/shared/box_scheduler/STEPS.tsv`, `harness/scripts/test_design_box_scheduler_steps.sh --list`): the bus (`harness/scripts/s4e_msg.sh`: rows that close, retire and escalate themselves, one verb per ruling, the RESUME verb, claim notices by touched paths, a done that holds no lock, the recut verb), the records (MODE capped with its archive, batons capped, the RULES index and the redirect map, a ruling found by its number), the landing (one landing command, generated tables merged by regeneration), hygiene (a background handle, a seat kills only its own, runners' outputs under /tmp), and the wake by hook. Not the grader (the coo), not the instruments and the box budget (the cqo), not the gates (the cao), not the box controller (the coo, steps 3 to 6). The rows: box-scheduler steps 8, 9, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 21, 22, 27, 28, 29, 30. Lon's escalation law binds this seat (CTO-498): a row it cannot finish is escalated to the cto the same sitting.

**Skills:** `/fleet-bus` first (every verb and its law), `/design-plans` (the step harness and the rows), `/shared-box` (the box and its DESIGN section 8), `/gates-and-witnesses` before editing any test or DONE-WHEN; the language skills only for a witness. The bus is a bash script under the harness rules; every verb keeps its one-line answer and its three exit codes.

- **CIO-34 (2026-11-10 08:00 CST, cio) -- A TEST HAS SIX PARTS, ITS PROGRAM, ITS ARGUMENTS, ITS STDIN, ITS STDOUT, ITS STDERR AND ITS EXIT STATUS; STDERR IS CAPTURED AND ANSWERED APART FROM STDOUT; EVERY ANSWER IS CUT AGAIN FROM THE ORACLE WITH THE STREAMS APART; CIO-24 (h) IS ANSWERED AND CIO-25 (a) AND CIO-32 (3) ARE EXTENDED.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:5x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "So you'll want to re-grade the programs using stdin (INPUT variable) and capturing stdout (OUTPUT variable) and stderr (TERMINAL variable in SNOBOL4). And also programs have command-line arguments, argc/argv. So that is another per-program entry."). MEASURED earlier today on corpus f141ec484: of 16399 table entries 346 are graded with stderr merged into the answer and every other entry's stderr is compared with nothing; arguments live in two places, the index's run_args column and ALL.argv; the exit status lives in ALL.wantrc. THE RULING. (1) THE SIX PARTS of a test: the program; its command-line arguments; its stdin (INPUT in SNOBOL4); its stdout (OUTPUT); its stderr (TERMINAL in SNOBOL4, the error stream in every language); its exit status. Merged stderr retires: Lon's open question of CIO-24 (h) is answered, the streams are apart. (2) IN corpus/tests, a MULTI.md section holds them as fences after its heading, each absent fence meaning empty: the language fence (the program), a fence tagged args with one argument a line (so an argument may hold a space), stdin, stdout, stderr; the exit status stays the index's column. A ONE-liner is a test with no arguments, no stdin, an empty stderr, exit status 0 and one line of stdout; any other test is in MULTI.md. ALL.argv and the run_args column fold into the args fence when a folder converts; the index's stderr column goes. (3) IN A PACKAGE, when its design resumes (CIO-33 b), the parts are files beside the program under its own name: NAME.args, NAME.stdin, NAME.stdout, NAME.stderr, an absent file meaning empty. (4) THE SCRIP RUNNER (CIO-32) reads the args fence and passes the arguments after the two dashes, and always writes NAME.out and NAME.err as two files. (5) THE RE-CUT: every entry's answer is cut again from its language's oracle with the three streams apart: the oracle run with the entry's stdin and arguments, its stdout and stderr captured as two streams, its exit status read, twice, the two runs equal or the entry named UNSTABLE. The fresh stdout and exit status must equal the stored answer; where they differ the entry is named DRIFTED with both, and its answer changes only by that named cut (this is also Lon's "let's not assume" applied to the answers themselves). The fresh stderr is stored as the new part. The 346 merged entries get their two streams from this cut. (6) STDERR IS COMPARED AS STDOUT IS, by the post step: byte for byte, and through the one error voice and the masks where the table declares them, since an engine's own diagnostic (its file name, its line, its wording) is compared by the rules that already compare it on stdout, never byte for byte. BEFORE IT COUNTS, THE MOVEMENT IS MEASURED: per language the coo runs the post step both ways, stdout and exit status alone and with stderr, and reports how many verdicts move and into which classes (a program's own TERMINAL text, an engine diagnostic, a note SCRIP alone prints); the cio brings that table to Lon and the stderr comparison becomes part of the grade by his word, so no suite turns red by a rule nobody measured. (7) THE ORDER inside a language, extending CIO-27 (11): absorb the loose files, regroup into ONE and MULTI.md (a pure change of form, the read-back identical), re-cut the answers with the streams apart, then the SCRIP switches and the post step. - **CIO-32 (2026-11-10 07:47 CST, cio) -- THE FORK STAYS: CIO-31 IS VOID; THE TWO SWITCHES ARE A READER IN FRONT OF THE EXISTING FORKED ROW RUNNER; THE DESIGN OF THE SWITCHES IS RESTATED WHOLE IN ONE PLACE.** THE WORD (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "You can keep the fork since it is fast and makes things so much simpler."), on the cio's measurement that the fork is about 0.2 ms of a 3.4 ms row. CIO-31 is void in whole: no warm-point snapshot, no wrapped exit, no self re-execution. THE DESIGN AS IT STANDS, superseding the pieces in CIO-26 to CIO-30 where they differ: (1) scrip --run-one-liners DIR --out OUT and scrip --run-multi-liners DIR --out OUT, with --only FILE for one cell's entries and --work DIR for scratch; --compile before either writes each test's assembly into OUT and runs nothing (mode 4 stays the Python grader's until Lon lifts mode 3 only). (2) THE READER: ALL.csv read by column name through a small quoted-cell reader; the Nth line of ONE.EXT is the Nth index row whose group is one; a section of MULTI.md is the row of its heading's name, fences line-anchored, a longer fence honoured, the language fence's tag giving the extension; a mismatch with the index refuses rc 2 before any test runs. SCRIP reads no answer: not ONE.stdout, not a stdout fence. (3) THE RUN: for each test whose road is direct, a fresh private directory under the work directory holding the program as the entry's last name component with its extension and its stdin block as a file; then the existing forked row runner, scrip_harness_row's mechanism: fork, the child's three streams set to the stdin file and to OUT's NAME.out and NAME.err (one file when the index says merged), the arguments exactly today's batch row (mode, the compile_args tokens, -dNk and -sNk, the source, two dashes, the run_args tokens), the child calling main anew, so every test's heap and stack are mapped fresh and nothing of one test reaches the next; the private directory removed after. A test whose road is staged is not run. (4) THE TIMEOUT is SCRIP's: timeout_s from the index, 120 when empty, the test's process group killed when it passes; the output bound (S4E_OUTPUT_CAP_MB, 256 when unset) set as the child's file-size limit. (5) THE REPORT, SCRIP's standard output, one tab-separated line a test in the order run: entry name, result type, a number, wall seconds, processor seconds, peak memory KB; the types EXIT (the exit status), ABORT (SIGABRT), CRASH (another signal, its number), TIMEOUT (killed at the limit with processor seconds at least a tenth of wall), HANG (under a tenth), OVERFLOW (SIGXFSZ), REFUSED (could not start, the errno), DEFERRED (staged, not run), each read by the parent from the wait status and the resource usage. SCRIP compares nothing, decides no pass or fail, takes no --record and writes no record or table. Exit 0 when every selected test was run or deferred; 2 refused. (6) THE POST STEP is the Python grader's, after the run: only an EXIT is compared with its answer (byte for byte, then the masks, compare tokens, want_rc and merged stderr the table declares); the other types are the outcome as they stand; then the progress rows and the suite row. Any seat may run the switches; only the post step is the board. (7) scrip --harness, its list and its record are unchanged by a byte until the old road is retired. (8) THE PROOF: the gate's arms for each result type and for a test not seeing the one before it; the same-verdict census on rebus in format 2, every entry's verdict by the switches and the post step equal to its verdict by today's road; then the seconds of both roads on the same box. (9) NEXT, not in this row: the fork taken from a parent that has already done a language's shared start (the Prolog base prelude). The row is the coo's: scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew, its NEXT rewritten to this text.
- **CIO-31 (2026-11-10 07:45 CST, cio) -- NO FORK: THE TWO SWITCHES RUN EVERY TEST IN ONE PROCESS AND RESET SCRIP TO ITS WARM POINT BETWEEN TESTS, THE HEAP AND STACK BY MMAP, THE STATIC DATA FROM A SNAPSHOT; AN ABNORMAL END IS FOLLOWED BY SCRIP RE-EXECUTING ITSELF; CIO-26 (a) AND (e) AND CIO-27 (1) AND (3) ARE AMENDED.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "It handles stdin, stdout, and stderr and a total reset of stack/heap using MMAP between each run." then "Do not use FORK."), after the cio recommended keeping the fork. MEASURED 07:4x on SCRIP 4d89b1093, the grader root's binary: the present engine runs 200 one-line SNOBOL4 rows with 200 clone calls at 225 microseconds each under strace, of 3.4 ms a row unstraced (500 rows, 1.72 s), so the fork is under a tenth of a row and the rest is each child's cold start and run; libscrip_rt.so carries 5.71 MB of data and 0.24 MB of bss, scrip 4.5 KB; the source has 87 exit and 19 _exit call sites, 169 abort sites, 4 pthread_create sites and 17 fork, system or popen sites in the runtime; the runtime maps its heap, its stack, its pinned region and its global window by anonymous mmap at start (gc_heap.c, rt_stack_overflow.c, rt.c). So dropping the fork saves little time by itself and what a fork gave for nothing must now be done by SCRIP: this ruling says how, and what makes it fast is the warm point, not the missing fork. THE DESIGN. (1) ONE PROCESS, A WARM POINT: scrip --run-one-liners and --run-multi-liners read the group and the index, then take the warm point before the first test: the list of the process's mappings, a copy of every writable mapping's bytes (the static data of scrip, libscrip_rt.so and the C library, about 6 MB measured, and the C heap up to its break), the open descriptors, the signal handlers and mask, the limits, the environment, the directory. The runner's own state (its place in the file, its output buffer) lives in a mapping it makes for itself and in its own frame, which the reset never touches; it writes its result lines with write on a descriptor it saved, never through stdio. (2) A TEST: the three streams are set by dup2 onto files (CIO-29), the directory by chdir, the timeout by a timer and the output bound by the file-size limit; the runner marks a return point with sigsetjmp and calls the driver's entry, the body of main made a function of its arguments. (3) THE FIVE ENDINGS all come back to the return point: exit, _exit and _Exit are wrapped at link in scrip and libscrip_rt.so (the 106 sites keep their text) and jump back as EXIT with the status; abort is wrapped and jumps back as ABORT; SIGSEGV, SIGBUS, SIGFPE and SIGILL are caught on an alternate signal stack as CRASH with the signal, after the runtime's own stack-overflow handler has had its turn; the timer's signal is TIMEOUT or HANG by the processor share (CIO-30); SIGXFSZ is OVERFLOW. (4) THE RESET AFTER AN EXIT, in order: the streams flushed and the runner's three descriptors restored, every descriptor not open at the warm point closed, the directory, handlers, mask, limits and environment put back; every mapping not present at the warm point unmapped, which is the total reset of heap and stack by mmap, the next test's start mapping them fresh at that test's declared sizes; every writable mapping of the warm point restored to its warm bytes. After it SCRIP is, byte for byte in its writable memory, the process it was before the test. The first build restores all of it each test and measures the cost; restoring only the pages a test wrote (write faults caught on protected pages) is the second step, taken only if the measurement asks for it. (5) AFTER ANY OTHER ENDING, AND WHEN A TEST LEFT A THREAD OR A CHILD PROCESS ALIVE, THE RUNNER DOES NOT TRUST AN IN-PROCESS RESET: a test cut at an arbitrary instruction can hold a lock inside the C library, and a live thread cannot be unmade from inside. It writes the test's line, kills the test's child processes, and RE-EXECUTES ITSELF with execve on its own binary and a resume index: that is not a fork, it makes no process, and the kernel replaces the whole image and ends every other thread. These endings are rare and cost one image load each. A run the kernel kills outright (out of memory, SIGKILL) ends there; the post step sees which test has no line and starts the runner again after it, charging that test KILLED. (6) scrip --harness and scrip_harness_row keep their fork untouched: they are the old road, removed with it once the census is green. (7) THE PROOF grows three arms beside the same-verdict census: ORDER, the group run forward, reversed and shuffled writes byte-identical files for every test; LEAK, after a thousand tests the runner's mappings, descriptors and resident memory are those after one; ENDINGS, an exit status, an abort, a segfault, a busy loop, a blocked read, a flood of output and a test that starts a thread each give their type and the next test passes. Then the seconds against the forked engine's 3.4 ms a row on the same box: the number that says what no fork bought. (8) NEXT, NOT IN THIS ROW: the warm point taken after a language's shared start (the Prolog base prelude, 4.37 G instructions a program today), which is where a warm process pays most. The row is the coo's: scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew; SKILLS scrip-architecture, scrip-c-code, scrip-build, runtime-calls, scrip-memory-model.
- **CIO-30 (2026-11-10 07:42 CST, cio) -- SCRIP REPORTS HOW EACH TEST ENDED AS A RESULT TYPE: EXIT, ABORT, CRASH, TIMEOUT, HANG, OVERFLOW, REFUSED, DEFERRED; CIO-29 (3) IS MADE EXACT.** THE WORD (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "Do have SCRIP report result-type, ABORT, CRASH, HANG, and TIMEOUT, etc."). MEASURED on SCRIP 4d89b1093: the runner today knows three endings, RAN, HANG (its timeout fired) and CRASH (any signal); SCRIP's source has 169 abort() sites (rt_bomb and the like), so a death by its own abort is a real class the word CRASH hides; wait4 hands the parent the child's processor seconds beside the wall seconds; the Python grader kills a test whose output passes 256 MB (S4E_OUTPUT_CAP_MB) and the runner has no such bound; the progress store's outcome words already hold ABORT, CRASH and HANG and read TIMEOUT as HANG. THE RULING: the run's line, one a test, tab separated, is the entry name, the RESULT TYPE, a number, the wall seconds, the processor seconds, the peak memory in KB. THE TYPES, each decided by the runner from what it alone observes, never from the test's output: EXIT, the program ran to an exit, the number its exit status; ABORT, it died on SIGABRT, SCRIP's own abort; CRASH, it died on any other signal the runner did not send, the number that signal; TIMEOUT, the runner killed it at timeout_s while it was working, its processor seconds at least a tenth of the wall seconds; HANG, the runner killed it at timeout_s while it was idle, under a tenth, blocked on a read, a lock or a sleep; OVERFLOW, its stdout or stderr passed the output bound (S4E_OUTPUT_CAP_MB, 256 when unset, set by the runner as the test's file-size limit) and the kernel stopped it; REFUSED, the runner could not start it (its directory, its files or the fork), the number the errno; DEFERRED, a staged test the runner does not run. For the four killed types the number is the signal. A type is never PASS or FAIL: the runner compares nothing (CIO-29). THE POST STEP reads the type first: only an EXIT is compared with the answer; ABORT, CRASH, TIMEOUT, HANG and OVERFLOW are the test's outcome as they stand, written to the progress store under its own words, TIMEOUT as HANG with the word in the note until the store's vocabulary gains TIMEOUT, which is the coo's one line in util_progress_append.py; REFUSED is could not measure, rc 2, never a red. The row is the coo's: scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew; its gate grows an arm for each type: an exit status, an abort, a segfault, a busy loop, a blocked read, a flood of output.
- **CIO-29 (2026-11-10 07:41 CST, cio) -- SCRIP IS THE RUNNER AND NOTHING MORE: IT READS THE TESTS, RUNS EACH ANEW WITH ITS STDIN, WRITES ITS STDOUT AND STDERR, AND ENFORCES THE TIMEOUT; IT COMPARES NO ANSWER AND WRITES NO RECORD; COMPARING AND RECORDING ARE THE POST STEP; CIO-26 (c), CIO-27 (6) AND (7) AND CIO-28 (1) ARE AMENDED.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim, in order: "Do not have SCRIP write the record. Make that a post step." / "Do not have SCRIP compare the answer." / "Do have SCRIP enforce the timeout."). THE RULING. (1) WHAT SCRIP DOES under scrip --run-one-liners DIR --out OUT and scrip --run-multi-liners DIR --out OUT: reads the tests from ONE.EXT or MULTI.md and their attributes from ALL.csv (CIO-27 (2) to (5), unchanged); for each direct test forks, runs the program from its private directory with its stdin block on standard input, its stdout and stderr written to OUT under the entry name with slashes as underscores (NAME.out and NAME.err; one file, NAME.out, when the index says stderr is merged); enforces the test's timeout_s from the index, 120 when the cell is empty, killing the test's whole process group when it passes. (2) WHAT SCRIP DOES NOT DO: it does not read ONE.stdout or a stdout fence, compares nothing, decides no pass or fail, writes no record file, no progress row and no table. --out is required: the files in OUT are the run's product. (3) THE ONE THING ONLY THE RUNNER KNOWS is how each test ended, so SCRIP prints it and nothing else, on its own standard output, one tab-separated line a test in the order run: the entry name, the exit status, RAN or HANG (the timeout fired) or CRASH (a signal) or DEFERRED (a staged test, not run), the seconds, the peak memory. Without that line no post step could tell a hang from a short answer or know the exit status; it is a report of the run, not the record. (4) THE EXIT CODE no longer speaks of answers: 0 when every selected test was run or deferred, whatever it printed; 2 refused (a file, a section or an --only name it cannot read), before any test runs. (5) THE POST STEP is the Python grader's, after the run has ended: for every test it reads OUT's files and the run's line, compares the stdout with the test's answer (ONE.stdout's line, the section's stdout fence) byte for byte first and through the masks, compare tokens, want_rc and merged stderr where the table declares them, reads HANG and CRASH from the line, and then writes the progress rows and the suite row. Comparing every test in one Python process over files already on disk is the cheap part; the 73 ms an entry of today's road is the staging and the launches, which are gone. (6) RUNNING IS FREE, RECORDING IS THE COO'S (CIO-28 (3) stands): any seat may run either switch; the post step alone is the board. (7) THE PROOF (CIO-27 (10)) reads the same: every entry's verdict by the switches plus the post step equals its verdict by today's road, then the seconds of both. The row is the coo's: scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew.
- **CIO-28 (2026-11-10 07:41 CST, cio) -- SCRIP RUNS AND REPORTS, IT RECORDS NOTHING: THE TWO SWITCHES WRITE NO RECORD FILE AND TOUCH NO TABLE; THE RECORD IS A POST STEP; CIO-26 (c) AND CIO-27 (6) ARE AMENDED.** THE WORD (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "Do not have SCRIP write the record. Make that a post step."), on the cio's sentence that SCRIP "compares the answer and writes the record". THE RULING. (1) scrip --run-one-liners and scrip --run-multi-liners take no --record and write no record file, no progress row and no table. Their whole product is the run's own standard output, one line a test in the order run, tab separated: the entry name, the exit status, RAN, HANG, CRASH or DEFERRED, the seconds, the peak memory, and SAME or DIFFERS (a dash when the test did not run to an exit); and, when --out DIR is given, the stdout and stderr files of every test that is not RAN, SAME and exit 0. The exit code is CIO-27 (7)'s. A run changes nothing outside --work and --out. (2) THE POST STEP is the Python grader's, after the run has ended, never interleaved with it: it reads that output, re-grades the tests that are not a clean pass through the masks, compare tokens, want_rc and merged stderr, and only then writes the per-test rows of the progress store and the suite row. A run that is killed leaves no half-written record: nothing was written. (3) WHAT FOLLOWS FROM IT: running is free and recording is the coo's. Any seat may run either switch over any container at any time, since a run writes no record; ONE RUNNER, ONE BOARD binds the post step alone, which only the cycle and the coo perform. (4) scrip --harness keeps its --record as it is; it is the old road and is retired with it. The row is the coo's: scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew.
- **CIO-27 (2026-11-10 07:38 CST, cio) -- UNDER DUO THE CIO DESIGNS THE SCRIP SIDE OF THE TWO SWITCHES AND THE COO BUILDS IT; THE SWITCHES ARE A READER AND A COMPARER AROUND THE EXISTING HARNESS ROW RUNNER; CIO-26 (a), (c) AND (g) ARE AMENDED; THE NEW CHAIN IS PROVED WHOLE ON REBUS FIRST.** THE WORD (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "We are in DUO mode, you, CIO, do the design COO does executions."), on the cio's ask for the cto: the cto is not woken; CIO-26 (g) is void and the row scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew is the coo's. Lon's question the same minute, "when you say harness you mean SCRIP executable": from here the record says SCRIP for the executable and its switches and the Python grader for harness/scripts/corpus_suite_harness.py. MEASURED on SCRIP 4d89b1093, scrip.c lines 1768 to 1942: scrip_harness_row takes one keyed row (name, stdin, stdout, stderr, cwd, env, timeout_s, switches, then the source path and its arguments), forks, and the child sets its directory and its three streams from the row and calls main again with the row's switches and source, so the language is chosen by the source file's extension and the program is read from that path; the parent waits with the timeout and writes the record row name, rc, kind, seconds, peak memory. The Python grader's batch road already tells a plain entry from one that needs a staged directory (_batch_m3_eligible, and a stage_entry that yields the candidate file alone) and composes the row's switches as the entry's compile switches, then -dNk and -sNk from heap_kb and stack_kb. THE DESIGN. (1) NO NEW ENGINE: scrip --run-one-liners DIR and scrip --run-multi-liners DIR are a reader in front of scrip_harness_row and a comparer behind it; scrip --harness, its list and its five-column record do not change by a byte, so the Python grader's present road stands untouched while the new one is proved. (2) THE READER: ALL.csv beside the files is read by column name through a small quoted-cell reader (a cell may be quoted and hold a comma); for --run-one-liners the Nth line of ONE.EXT and of ONE.stdout is the Nth row whose group is one; for --run-multi-liners a section of MULTI.md is the row of its heading's name, its fences read line-anchored, a fence of more than three backticks honoured, the language fence's tag giving the extension (SNOBOL4 sno, Icon icn, Prolog pl, Raku raku, Pascal pas, Snocone sc, Rebus reb); a line count that differs from the index, a heading the index lacks or a section with no stdout fence refuses rc 2 naming it, before any test runs. (3) THE PROGRAM IS A FILE IN A PRIVATE DIRECTORY, AMENDING CIO-26 (a)'s from memory: a program prints its own file name and opens files beside itself, and main chooses the language by the extension, so for each test SCRIP makes a fresh directory under --work DIR (default a directory of its own under TMPDIR), writes the program there as the entry's last name component with the extension, its stdin block as a file, and runs the row with that directory as cwd and its stdout and stderr to files there; the directory is removed after the test. The cost is a few file operations a test; nothing is staged by Python. (4) THE ROAD COLUMN: the index gains road, direct or staged, written by regroup and by every tool that adds an entry, decided by the Python grader's own present rule (the entry is batch eligible and its staging yields its one file: no cwd, no companion, no out_files, no stage, no grader); SCRIP runs a direct test and records a staged one as DEFERRED without running it; the Python grader runs the DEFERRED ones on today's staged road. A one-liner is direct by construction. (5) THE ARGUMENTS of a direct test are exactly today's row for the same entry: mode, the compile_args cell's tokens, -dNk and -sNk from heap_kb and stack_kb, the source, then two dashes and the run_args tokens; timeout_s from the index, 120 when empty, the Python grader's default. (6) THE COMPARER: after the wait the parent compares the stdout file with the test's stdout block, sizes first, then bytes; the record row is the five columns and a sixth, SAME or DIFFERS, or a dash for a test that did not run to an exit. A test that is RAN, SAME and rc 0 leaves nothing; with --out DIR every other test's stdout and stderr files are moved there under the entry name with slashes as underscores, as --harness names them, for the Python grader's masks, compare tokens, want_rc and merged stderr. (7) SELECTION AND EXIT: --only FILE, one entry a line, a name the group lacks refusing rc 2; exit 0 when every selected direct test is RAN, SAME, rc 0 and none is DEFERRED, 1 otherwise, 2 refused. (8) MODE 4: --compile before either switch writes each direct test's assembly into --out DIR as --compile --harness does and compares nothing; the full mode-4 road stays the Python grader's until Lon lifts mode 3 only. (9) THE CODE LAW binds the row (skill scrip-c-code): 200 columns, no comment, no blank line, the arena and never an allocator, no new global, no fixed limit a file can reach (a container of any size, a line of any length); the reader lives in the driver. (10) THE PROOF IS A SAME-VERDICT CENSUS: on a master in format 2, every entry's verdict by the two switches plus the Python grader's re-grade equals its verdict by today's staged road, entry for entry, in mode 3; then the seconds of both roads are read on the same quiet box, which is the measurement of Lon's "This would be very fast." (11) THE ORDER, amending CIO-24's: ONE LANGUAGE END TO END AT A TIME, smallest first. Rebus first (43 entries, 7 loose files): its loose files absorbed, its container regrouped into ONE and MULTI.md, the two switches built and proved on it, the Python grader calling them for its cells. Then snocone, snobol4, pascal, raku, prolog, icon, each absorbed, regrouped and switched in turn; the packages after the masters. The rows in flight (inria, logtalk) finish first.
- **CIO-26 (2026-11-10 07:35 CST, cio) -- SCRIP READS THE CONTAINER'S TWO FILES ITSELF AND RUNS EACH TEST ANEW: SCRIP --RUN-ONE-LINERS AND SCRIP --RUN-MULTI-LINERS; THE CONTRACT IS THE CIO'S, THE SCRIP SIDE THE CTO'S.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "Could we not have two --harness type features in SCRIP to read these file and run each anew. This would be very fast. SCRIP can run each one liner directly from the file and also do the same for the SCRIPtix file. How about command-line switches: --run-one-liners and --run-multi-liners."). MEASURED on SCRIP 4d89b1093: the engine exists. scrip --harness LIST (scrip.c, scrip_harness_cli and scrip_harness_main; DESIGN-PLAN batch-grader, CTO-515 and CTO-522) runs every row of a keyed list in one process, each row a fork from the warm parent with its own stdin, stdout, stderr, timeout and directory, and writes a record row (rc; RAN, HANG, CRASH or REFUSED; seconds; peak memory); a fatal, a hang or a crash is that row's record and the list goes on. What it does not do is read a container: the Python harness extracts every entry to a file on disk, writes the list, and reads every stdout file back to grade it. By that road lap 9 graded, in mode 3, sno-rungs 2211 entries in 161 s, raku-rungs 1390 in 128 s, icn-rungs 956 in 65 s, pas-rungs 385 in 56 s, snc-rungs 358 in 28 s and pl-rungs 584 in 439 s (CYCLE.log); the gain of the direct road is not measured and is the row's step 0. THE RULING. (a) TWO SWITCHES, Lon's names: scrip --run-one-liners DIR reads ONE.EXT and ONE.stdout of a container directory; scrip --run-multi-liners DIR reads MULTI.md (CIO-24 f, CIO-25); --compile before either is mode 4, as --harness has it. Each is the --harness engine with a reader in front: for every test a fork from the warm parent, the program text handed over from memory under the name ENTRY.EXT, its stdin block on standard input, nothing staged on disk. (b) THE ATTRIBUTES come from ALL.csv beside the files, read by column name: heap_kb, stack_kb, timeout_s, compile_args, run_args, cwd; a column the table lacks is the default. (c) THE COMPARISON IS IN SCRIP AND IS BYTE FOR BYTE: the parent captures the child's stdout and compares it with the test's stdout block; the record row gains SAME or DIFFERS. That is not yet the grade: a test graded through a mask, a compare token, a nonzero want_rc, merged stderr or written files is decided by the Python grader, so with --out DIR scrip writes the actual stdout and stderr of every test that is not RAN, SAME and rc 0, and of no other; the grader re-grades only those. A passing test costs no file and no Python. (d) SELECTION: --only FILE names the entries of one cycle cell; without it the whole group runs. Exit 0 when every selected test is RAN, SAME, rc 0; 1 otherwise; 2 refused with the file or section named. (e) A TEST NEVER SEES THE ONE BEFORE IT: the fork is the reset, as batch-grader step 2 proved it; the warm parent holds only what every test of the file shares (the runtime, and for Prolog the base prelude, batch-grader step 8, where an empty program costs 4.37 G instructions today). (f) THE CYCLE: a cell of a container in format 2 is two launches, one a group, in place of one launch of corpus_suite_harness.py over staged files; the harness keeps the selection, the record's reading, the re-grade of the DIFFERS rows and the progress rows. (g) WHO: the SCRIP side is the cto's design and row, scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew (minted, owner cto, FREE); the cto is idle under DUO and a telegram wakes nobody, so the cio asked Lon for the word to add the cto to the live set. The harness side is the coo's, after it. ORDER: format 2 lands first on one master (rebus, 43 entries) so the switches have a file to read; the two then go forward together.
- **CIO-25 (2026-11-10 07:33 CST, cio) -- THE MULTI-LINER GROUP IS A MARKDOWN FILE IN SCRIPTIX'S OWN FENCE SYNTAX, ONE SECTION A TEST, ITS PROGRAM, STDIN AND STDOUT TOGETHER; CIO-24 (f) IS AMENDED.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:3x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "How about storing the multi liners in a SCRIPtix MD file using the triple-backticks for a clean delineation." / "This way the files can be viewed nicely."). MEASURED 07:3x on corpus f141ec484 and SCRIP 4d89b1093: (1) a Scriptix file is fenced blocks tagged with the language (SNOBOL4, Icon, Prolog, Raku, Pascal, Snocone, Rebus, SCRIP; polyglot.c), and scrip runs one .md as ONE polyglot program, every block linked with the others (tests/scrip_test/cross_lang.md: a SNOBOL4 block calls an Icon and a Prolog block); (2) three backticks occur on 6 lines of all the containers, none in the seven masters: rosetta-prolog (3 program lines, 2 answer lines) and xsb_tests (1 line, which holds four); (3) polyglot.c finds the opening and the closing fence with strstr anywhere in the text, not at a line start; (4) the multi-line entries sit in hundreds of families of one or two entries (snobol4 518 families for 1400 entries), so a file per family is 518 files; (5) the largest master is 1.13 MB of programs and 0.44 MB of answers (snobol4). THE RULING, replacing MULTI.EXT, MULTI.stdout and MULTI.stdin of CIO-24 (f); everything else of CIO-24 stands, the one-liner group included (ONE.EXT and ONE.stdout, line for line). (a) THE FILE: MULTI.md in the container directory. A test is ONE SECTION: a heading line of two hashes and the entry's name exactly as ALL.csv has it; then the program in a fence tagged with the language as Scriptix spells it; then, when the test has input, a fence tagged stdin; then a fence tagged stdout. A test's three parts sit together, so no file is aligned with another by position, a reader sees the program beside its answer, and a viewer highlights it. Text between a heading and its first fence is the test's own description, kept and never graded. (b) THE FENCE is a line that is only backticks and the tag: three, or one more than the longest run of backticks inside the block (Markdown's own rule), so the 6 lines are held without escaping; the reader and writer are line-anchored. A block's bytes run from the line after the opening fence to the newline before the closing fence; a program or an answer whose last line has no newline is told by the index (a column the converter writes only where it is so), since the read-back must be byte for byte. (c) IT IS SCRIPTIX SYNTAX, NOT ONE SCRIPTIX PROGRAM: the harness extracts a section and runs its program as it runs an entry today; scrip is never handed MULTI.md whole. A section may hold more than one language fence, and is then one polyglot test run as a .md: that is the home Scriptix's own tests never had under corpus/tests. polyglot.c's strstr fence is SCRIP's and the cto's: told, not changed here. (d) SIZE AND VIEWING: one MULTI.md a container; when the coo measures that the largest does not render where Lon views it, the file is cut into MULTI.01.md, MULTI.02.md and so on in index order at a fixed count of tests, new tests appended to the last; never a file per family. (e) THE INDEX AND THE TABLES do not change: ALL.csv with its group column, PARTITION.tsv, ALL.wantrc, ALL.argv, ALL.mask. (f) THE MOVE is CIO-24 (f)'s: the reader takes a container directory in any of the formats, regroup converts one directory and refuses unless the read-back gives the same entries in order with the same program, answer and stdin bytes, the masters first. The row is the coo's, re-pointed by this ruling: tests-layout-the-container-is-two-groups-one-liner-and-multi-liner-with-stdin-and-stdout-named.

Twelve more worktrees belong to rows their seat no longer holds (escalated to another seat, re-queued to a tube, parked BLOCKED-ON; idle 35 to 390 minutes: senior_03 wt-ab and wt-s5b, senior_01's two, junior_07's four, junior_16 wt-junior16-swi-wfs, junior_06, junior_10, junior_12 wt-hdr5) and two are named by no baton (coo wt-unify, grader wt-req-1); the sweep's rule never reaches them. officer_07's three (wt-aoc, wt-gap, wt-self, idle 540 to 618 minutes) carry no marker and are not counted, but hold 2 GB. THE RULING: (1) the unlanded guard measures against origin/main, not against the index: an edit whose content origin/main already carries is landed, not dirty; (2) the sweep has three dead classes with idle floors, DEAD-DONE 30 minutes (today's), DEAD-LEFT 60 minutes (the row's state names another holder or none), DEAD-UNNAMED 120 minutes (no baton names it), each with the no-process guard, and before any rm it writes the worktree's patch against origin/main, untracked files included, to /home/resources/postoffice/escalations/worktrees/<seat>-<name>-<stamp>.patch and names it in the row's LEDGER, so nothing a seat wrote is lost; (3) the sweep runs at every new, before the cap check, not only at the cap; (4) WT_MAX's default is 40 tonight (40 times 0.8 GB fits the free /tmp with 20 GB to spare; the sweep working, the standing count falls to the live rows and the cap is never the bound); a wider cap is a disk question, not a queue question. THE ROWS: the mechanism (1) to (3) with its probe is fleet-worktree-sweep-reclaims-landed-escalated-and-unnamed-worktrees-and-saves-the-unlanded-patch, rank 1 in tier-haiku (junior_09 woken; its own dead worktree is the first it removes); the cap line (4) the cio lands tonight under fleet-worktree-cap-default-40. A seat's own dead worktree it removes itself with rm --discard once its row is DONE or has left it; a seat removes no other seat's. The hand removal of other seats' worktrees was refused to the cio by its permission classifier and is not retried: the sweep removes as the owner, and that is the one road.

(tabling/test_wfs.pl, three files against the concatenation: the same case lines), so an SWI file is one container entry in the banner-block format. (2) The score-row writer already counts SWI per case: util_score_row.py from-progress has the swi road (SUITE_ROW_SWI, _swi_case_census over util_swi_match.py's enumeration) and the live progress table carries the old runner's rows as class package, suite swi, program <file>.pl:<unit>:<test>#k, note "case of <file>.pl" (results.tsv, grader rows of 2026-11-08 20:25). So the one gap is the harness: it grades an entry by stdout equality and writes one row per entry. (3) The harness reads its attributes from ALL.csv by column name in read_command_line_columns (compile_args, run_args, out_files, stderr, compare, stage, answer, mode_gap_s) and records one progress row per entry and mode at the one place in cmd_run where _progress_rows.append runs; a column it does not know is ignored, so a builder may write the new columns before the harness reads them. (4) swi_tests has ALL.csv (162 file rows with heap_kb, stack_kb, timeout_s) and PARTITION.tsv (core, chunk:01, chunk:02) but no ALL.pl and no ALL.ref; every file's oracle verdicts sit beside it as <file>.ref. (5) The IPL refusal is not a missing driver road but a stale partition: ipl's ALL.csv has 851 rows, ALL.excluded.txt 773 of them, ALL.icn 78 entries, and util_suite_partition.py builds the chunks over every ALL.csv row, so chunk:01 to 08 name excluded programs the container does not hold (the harness refuses "N of its entries are not in"); arizona (135 rows, 107 entries, 41 excluded, 37 of them drivers that load C through .clib) and gimpel (145 rows, 144 entries) refuse the same way. (6) ipl ships 642 refs beside its sources and 362 NAME_driver.icn files; the deleted runner graded a library through its driver under the library's name (R-1269) with the driver's directory on IPATH; ipl has no CONTRACT.tsv, so the builder absorbed the 78 programs only. THE RULING. (a) THE GRADER ATTRIBUTE: ALL.csv gains the column grader, empty or text (today's stdout equality), swi-cases or logtalk-cases; for a cases grader the harness runs the entry once per mode as today, hands its stdout, exit status, the entry's ref block and the entry's origin source to the package's matcher (util_swi_match.py; util_logtalk_grade.py's verdict for Logtalk), and records ONE PROGRESS ROW PER CASE named <entry>.pl:<case key> with the matcher's word (PASS, FAIL, UNGRADED, CRASH, HANG are all in util_progress_append's vocabulary) and the note "case of <entry>.pl", counts each case in the board tallies, and prints one line per entry (SWI_CASES <entry> cases=N hit=H miss=M ungraded=U); the entry's own row is not written; the entry reads red when any case misses. The suite row then comes from the writer's existing swi road, unchanged. (b) THE CWD ATTRIBUTE: ALL.csv gains the column cwd, a directory under the container's; when set, the harness runs the entry (both modes) from that directory with the staged source by absolute path, because plunit files open data beside them (files/test_glob.pl) and the deleted runner ran each file from its own directory. (c) THE SWI CONTAINER: CONTRACT.tsv beside swi_tests declares units plunit; the builder's plunit mode makes one entry per .pl that declares begin_tests and has a .ref beside it (CONTAINERS.tsv files skipped; a .pl without a .ref excluded by name), its text the shim, the file and the wrapper, its ALL.ref block the beside .ref verbatim, its ALL.csv row the existing attributes plus grader swi-cases and cwd its directory; PARTITION.tsv is rebuilt and stays three cells. (d) LOGTALK: the same hook with grader logtalk-cases over a container of one entry per case (its ALL.csv already lists 3617 cases) whose text is util_logtalk_grade.program_text's program and whose ref block carries the case's want (succ, fail or the ball term) so verdict() decides from the run's outcome; a design the row measures first on one group. (e) A PARTITION NAMES ONLY CONTAINER ENTRIES: util_suite_partition.py build drops the rows ALL.excluded.txt and CONTAINERS.tsv name, so the harness's partition refusal cannot fire on a consistent package; the three partitions are rebuilt. (f) A LIBRARY THROUGH ITS DRIVER: ipl gets CONTRACT.tsv units ref-beside and ipath procs and gprocs (the key repeated, the builder joining them), the builder absorbs NAME_driver.icn with NAME_driver.ref as entry NAME (R-1269: the driver runs, the library is counted), and the harness exports IPATH from the container's CONTRACT.tsv ipath rows into the run and compile environment of every entry of that container, nothing else; arizona's 37 .clib drivers stay excluded by their named reason. (g) THE ELEVEN CONTAINERLESS STDOUT SUITES (snoflake dotnet testpgms inria fpc pat roast x64tests x32tests and the two above) are builds, not designs: util_build_package_suite.py <pkg> --lang L per package, each a row the coo or officer_08 (the records and cycle owner, CIO-12) mints with the builder's refusal as its measurement; the cio minted none of them. THE ROWS, tier-haiku, SWI first: suite-swi-container-one-plunit-entry-per-file-built-by-the-package-builder-under-contract-units-plunit (c, with b's column written); suite-harness-grader-attribute-swi-cases-expands-an-entry-into-its-case-rows-and-cwd-runs-it-from-its-directory (a and b; BLOCKED-ON the container); suite-harness-grader-attribute-logtalk-cases-and-the-logtalk-container-of-one-entry-per-case (d; BLOCKED-ON the grader hook); suite-partition-build-drops-the-excluded-and-container-rows-so-every-partition-entry-is-a-container-entry (e); suite-ipl-container-absorbs-the-driver-graded-libraries-under-contract-units-ref-beside-and-ipath (f; after e). WHEN the first two land, the cycle's next lap grades swi core and its two chunks and the writer's swi road rewrites the SWI row; the coo reads it. NOT DONE by the cio: no file changed; the rows carry the measurements, the exact columns, the call sites and the proofs.
- **CIO-14 (2026-11-09 01:46 CST, cio) -- THE THREE SWI DESIGNS ESCALATED TO TIER-FABLE ARE SPLIT BETWEEN THE TWO FABLE SEATS; THE CIO'S DESIGN OF THE TABLING-WFS ROW (THREE MEASURED CAUSES, THREE HAIKU ROWS) AND OF THE GLOB ROW (THE DEFERRED IF BRANCH INSIDE A UNIT JOINS THE OPEN UNIT); THE ROUTING RULE FOR THE COO.** LON'S WORD (2026-11-08 23:5x, in the coo's window, relayed by COO-497 and the coo's escalations of 01:1x): "Get SWI to 100%." THE SPLIT (01:1x CST): the coo escalated four SWI designs to tier-fable in one hour (strings, tabling-wfs, files-glob, test_reload) with "pass it to the cto if his"; both Fable seats are alive, so the cio took tabling-wfs (job 6235) and files-glob (6238) and assigned strings (6241, seat-cto) and reload (tier-fable) to the cto beside his prolog_clause row. THE ROUTING RULE from here, so the coo asks nobody per item: a tier-fable SWI design about the reader, the lowerer, the runtime, the clause database or module resolution wakes the CTO; one about the tabling family (tabling.pl, WFS, moded and incremental tables) or the grading and plunit shim shape wakes the CIO. THE TABLING-WFS DESIGN, every cause MEASURED by the cio 01:2x to 01:4x on SCRIP db9e1f5bb with probes in /home/resources/postoffice/escalations/probes/pl_tabling_cio14_*.pl against swipl 9.0.4: (a) dwin#1: both move orders lose the delay (dw.pl and dw2.pl print true, dw3.pl prints 2-true where swipl prints 2-win_cnt(0,_) and dwin(0)): the conditional contribution to a moded aggregate always completes LAST because its negative consumer parks, and the prelude's '$tbl_sl_mins'/5 writes the delay list mu(M,D) only when the answer skeleton is new (E==0); a later conditional contribution goes through '$tbl_mput'/6 which keeps the old delays or none. CURE: a contribution that changes the aggregate appends its delays to the entry's delays (the entry becomes mu(New, Old ++ D)); an unconditional contribution with the same value as a conditional entry strips the delays; the scheduler's unconditional count ('$tbl_sl' op 7) is flagged only when the written entry is unconditional; the entry is replaced by delete and insert so a parked consumer reading by slot sees it. Row prolog-tabling-a-moded-answer-keeps-the-delays-of-every-contribution-that-changed-it. (b) as#1: NOT a moded fault. The plain table puas(1,X) :- not_exists(puas(1,X)) gives the same empty residual (b1.pl), and the structure p :- tnot(q), q :- p works (b9, b10, b11), as does a program-defined tabled wrapper tc(G) :- call(G) in place of tabled_call (b16). The fault is the prelude's tabled_call/1: '$tnot_impl'/2 resolves the implementation by current_predicate('$tbl tabled_call'/1), which is false from the user module (b17.pl prints tc_no, impl_fail), so tnot/1 falls off the SL scheduler onto the old engine's tnot road, which succeeds with no delay (b14 prints _G0-true and an empty residual; b15, the ground form, fails outright). CURE: one clause, '$tnot_impl'(tabled_call(G),'$tbl tabled_call'(G)):-!., beside the existing undefined/0 clause. Row prolog-tabling-tnot-resolves-the-preludes-tabled-call-so-not-exists-of-a-non-ground-goal-delays. MEASURED beside it and left as is: swipl answers a moded aggregate whose last contribution was unconditional as unconditional (b4.pl: 5-true where SCRIP keeps the delay); no suite case reads it. (c) reeval#1: the probe reeval.pl agrees with swipl on 17 of 18 lines; the last differs: after assertz(d(0)) and the re-evaluation of p to the same answer set [1], swipl reads p2 valid (the manual, tabling-incremental: an unchanged re-evaluation decrements the invalid count of the affected tables) and SCRIP reads it invalid, because '$tbl_valid'/4 drops an invalid table and re-evaluates into a fresh trie with no comparison and no word to the dependents. CURE in tabling.pl alone: '$tbl_valid' hands the old trie as old(T0); '$tbl_enter'(old(T0),...) creates the fresh table with the idg's Forced field carrying old(T0) for an incremental table (the field is never read for incr; mono and lazy keep false); at completion ('$tbl_complete', '$tbl_done', '$tbl_fin_all') a hook '$tbl_cmpl'/1 compares the two answer sets by '$tbl_chg'/3: equal, the dependents' Deps are rebound from T0 to T and every dependent that is invalid, not dirty, and whose every table dependency is complete is set complete again, recursively; changed, the dependents are rebound and marked dirty (Forced=dirty, cleared when they re-evaluate); T0 is destroyed. The SL road ('$tbl_sl_get', '$tbl_sl_nget') maps old(_) to none. The dirty mark is the falsecount: a dependent is valid again only when no dependency is invalid and none changed since its last evaluation. Row prolog-tabling-an-unchanged-incremental-re-evaluation-revalidates-its-dependents. THE GLOB DESIGN (2), MEASURED on the probe pl_deferred_if_in_unit_u1.pl: swipl runs both tests; SCRIP prints a permission_error(modify,static_procedure,test/1) warning, the unit banner twice and runs test a twice, because '$cc_consult'/3 (the consult prelude in prolog_parse.c, line 3164) wraps the deferred branch in a fresh begin_tests/end_tests (the shim's begin_tests adds the suite a second time) and feeds it to '$consult_term', which asserts test(b) against the compiled unit's static test/1. CURE: a branch inside a unit is read by the unit loader road the load_files prelude already has ('$load_items'/3, which turns test/1 and test/2 clauses into pj_test/4 facts qualified by plunit_U), with no begin_tests wrapper; and '$load_one' (line 3957) stops asserting the test clause itself (pj_test is what run_tests reads). Row prolog-plunit-a-deferred-if-branch-inside-a-unit-joins-the-open-unit-through-the-unit-loader; the cyrillic and file_names reds under the :- if are its acceptance. The ls/2 reds of file_names 1 and 6 go to a junior as a probe row under COO-497 (prolog-swi-file-names-ls-2-cases-1-and-6-reproduced-named-and-cured-or-escalated). The two umbrella rows the cio holds park BLOCKED-ON their rows. NOT DONE: nothing in SCRIP changed by the cio; the rows carry the exact clause texts and the probes.

- **CIO-11 (2026-11-08 12:46 CST, cio) -- CIO-11 (2026-11-08 12:4x CST, cio) -- THE FLEET REVIEWED FROM 10:4x TO 12:4x UNDER CIO-8 AND CIO-10: WHAT HELD, WHAT BROKE, AND THE WORKLIST FOR LON TO WALK; NOTHING QUEUED BY THIS RULING.** LON'S WORD (in the cio's window 12:3x, verbatim): "go check on how your fleet is operating under your design and make note of the failures and success. go back to when you went to sleep until now and see what you need to fix with the fleet. SO: this ruling is the review and the worklist; it mints, queues and wakes nothing; each item becomes rows only when Lon says walk. WHAT HELD (measured 12:3x to 12:4x). (1) THE ONE THING MOVED: UniTests mode 3 went 101 to 115 of 163 since the morning (SUITES.history.tsv 17:18Z 105 blended, 18:23Z 115 m3; the grid reads 115/163), fourteen entries in two hours. (2) THE OFFICERS DESIGNED: 40 harness landings since 10:30 (git log harness), among them officer_05's load design parts 1 and 2 (the fleet cap 14, the pools summing to it, 2371d385), its interim guard (b5047609) and the holder fix it cut for junior_04 (4b3c63ae 12:32), officer_06's harness-road family (7c0, 7c1, 10a, 10c), bus-one-queue steps 1 and 9 (2c15684e, 8ea28a73), the SWI runner reading S4E_MODES (36e6d801); the coo measured the officers' cuts escalating at 11 percent (12:28) against 71 percent in the morning. (3) THE WAKE WORKS: ListAgents at 12:3x read 24 of 28 sessions busy and 4 idle (JUNIOR-04, OFFICER-08, SENIOR-01, CTO); tier-haiku ready 0, every ready job taken; nobody waited on the coo for a wake. (4) PROBLEMS FLOWED UP: 24 escalation files since 10:40 (14 rung 2, 8 rung 3, 2 rung 4), none to tier-fable (ready 0), so the seniors and officers absorbed every failure; the three false SUPERSEDED rows of the morning became PARKED-PREMISE-RED by CIO-9 and none recurred. (5) THE CAP HELD THE BOX once applied: the coo set s4e.slice 14 cores, seats 12, grader 4, batch 2 at 12:11 on Lon's go, and CPU PSI avg10 fell from 71 (12:02) to 25 (12:35). WHAT BROKE (measured). F1 THE RING: one tier-haiku job served to many juniors and every taker archiving the real holder's claim as STALE: job 5665 step 44 five takers, job 532 step 28 eight, job 5702 step 50 six, u7a four, step 55 three; 30 STALE CLAIM ledger lines today; five step-28 worktrees, four wt-s44, three wt-step50; the worktree cap 32 of 32 filled at 12:19 and blocked the true holder. CAUSE (officer_05 13:1x, read by me in util_queue.py line 220 and s4e_msg.sh lines 473 to 476): the reserve is not continuous. next's connection closes after the claim write so the job is READY again; reserve-hold must win it back against every other seat's next; the seat that wins archives the holder's claim under the head-never-cycles rule of 2026-11-07, and the robbed holder's hold reads "claim names another seat: release", which rings the next seat. The 12:32 fix keeps the hold through a 600 s grace when the foreign claim names the same job; the take side still archives, so the ring is slowed, not cut. F2 THE LOAD: at 12:02 load 34 to 40 and PSI avg10 71 with 14 juniors and 8 officers; s4e.slice cpu.max read max, each seat 400 percent (56 cores of quota on 16), the controller down since 08:48 (s4e-tick.timer stopped by a systemctl stop at 08:48:07 CST that was not Lon's, kept down by the cio's order in CIO-6; CIO-13), so no actuator moved anything until the coo's hand cap. Now load 24 to 26 on 16 cores, PSI some avg10 25 avg60 33: still oversubscribed under throttle. The top consumers outside every pool at 12:40 (ps): git rev-list at 300 percent (next's pre-premise sync), the landers' two table checks at 64 percent each (util_gen_rt_entry_table --check-alloc, util_gen_gc_allocating_table --check: CIO-8 took them OFF the run pool, which freed them instead of fencing them; officer_05 is right that they belong inside a pool), the coo's by-hand UniTests suite at 90 percent. F4 ONE RUNNER: officer_06's 12 UniTests verdicts (11:2x) reached no results.tsv row because it ran as officer_06; CIO-10 (a) answered that a by-hand verdict runs as seat grader, but no UniTests NEXT says so, so the answer lives in a ruling and not in the row the junior reads. W3 THE CHECKS INSIDE A POOL (F2): the landers' table checks take a compile slot, and next's pre-premise sync runs git with pack.threads 1; the per-seat quota stays 400 percent because the slice cap, not the seat quota, is the flexible bound for any N. W4 A TELEGRAM WAKES A WAITING SEAT (F5): next --wait returns when the seat's inbox gains a file, so the Stop gate's loop reads mail; one wait loop in s4e_msg.sh; it ends the idle-officer-with-unread-mail class without a second wake road. W5 THE BANNER (F5): in flight, junior_05; no new row. W6 THE GRADER LINE IN EVERY UNITESTS NEXT (F4): the cutters' template gains the line "the by-hand verdict runs as seat grader, mode 3" with the exact command; the lane question waits for the cycle. W7 THE EIGHT STRANDED ROWS (F6): re-queue each QUEUED:seat-<old> row to its renamed seat's tube by the rename map (cao to officer_06, manager to senior_08, president to senior_05, worker_08 to junior_08; the seat-cto three stay for the cto's waking); the buried jobs on dead tubes and the grade tube go when phase two retires beanstalkd, not before. W8 ONE HOLDER PER SEAT (F7): reserve-hold exits when stats-job answers NOT_FOUND and refuses to start beside a live holder of the same seat (a pidfile under /tmp/s4e/<seat>/). W9 SPLIT OFFICER_05'S PORTFOLIO (F8): the bus and the queue stay with officer_05; the cycle and the records go to officer_08; the box and the load to officer_07 once its UniTests general reds are cut; a MODE line and a CIO ruling, no code. W10 FLEXIBLE TIERS (F9): the live set is the seat scopes, so MODE's FLEET line names no seats by hand (FLEET: IDLE still stops all; a seat is live iff its scope holds a claude process; lib_wake already reads it so); TIERS.tsv gains a per-tier default row for effort with the seat rows as overrides, and spawn_fleet.sh (Lon's) takes --count <tier>=N and --effort <tier>=E so a tier is resized or retuned in one line; the cap is recomputed from the live count by util_fleet_settings.sh at each spawn. W11 PHASE TWO CONTINUES (bus-one-queue 9 steps: 1 and 9 landed, 2 to 6 and 8 open): it is the root cure of F1, F6 and F7 and the only item that removes beanstalkd's reserve window. REPLIED THIS SITTING: junior_02's defect on job 5702 (the loop is cured on origin at 4b3c63ae for the hold side; step 50 stays with junior_11; junior_02 saves its wt-esc-chain diff beside the escalation file and removes the worktree). - **CIO-10 (2026-11-08 11:37 CST, cio) -- CIO-10 (2026-11-08 11:4x CST, cio) -- THE OFFICERS DESIGN; THE CIO AND THE CTO SLEEP UNTIL AN ASK OR AN ESCALATION REACHES TIER-FABLE; A FLEET DEFECT GOES TO AN OFFICER, NEVER TO THE CIO FIRST; THE NEW TEAM KEYED; THE LAST FOUR CURES LANDED.** LON'S WORD (in the cio's window, 11:3x to 11:4x, verbatim): "I really want to see these Fable seats sleeping. Finish design and sleep. Give more to officers." / "CIO and CTO need to slow down and delegate more to officers." / "So then the system is out of whack and depends on the CTO/CIO WAY TOO much. The officers must step up for design. Hello." / "Now!!!" THE RULE: (1) An officer (Opus) designs: a fleet defect with its measurement goes by telegram to officer_05 (the bus, the queue, the lander, the cycle, the records; its row is CIO-8 phase two) or to the UniTests officer whose step it touches, and that officer designs the cure, cuts it under THE HAIKU BAR and queues it; the coo routes a defect to the officer, never to the cio. (2) The cio and the cto are reached only by ask (ask-fable) or by an escalation that reaches tier-fable, and only after the officer has written its own proposal in the ask so that a yes settles it (officer_05's eight questions of 11:0x are the model). (3) The cio answers an ask with the answer verb and goes back to sleep; it lands code only on a fleet question no officer can answer. (4) The cto the same for SCRIP. LANDED SINCE CIO-9 (harness 710f57f1e, 0a52a1a5e): next syncs all four repos and runs make -j8 (a cache fetch) before it reads a premise, refusing when make fails; a RED premise at one root PARKS the row PARKED-PREMISE-RED:<reader>@<scrip tree> with the reader's four trees in the LEDGER, it no longer retires it (three false SUPERSEDED today; the designer re-reads on origin, recuts if wrong, queues again); the answer verb releases the identity before its done leg (the cto's three refusals); the refused-head probe names junior_20 and is the landing's gate; util_score_row.py from-progress grades a row in m3 alone under S4E_MODES=m3, which the cycle exports, so the UniTests headline reads 110 of 163, the m3 count, not the blended 105 (the coo 11:29). ANSWERED, NO CODE: (a) the ONE RUNNER lane (the coo 11:25): a UniTests verdict by hand is taken as seat grader exactly as CIO-7 wrote it (S4E_HOME=/home/claude_grader S4E_SEAT=grader S4E_CLASS=grader S4E_BATCH=1 S4E_CHUNK=entry:<name> bash harness/scripts/test_icon_unicon_suite.sh, mode 3); a roster seat's reading never reaches results.tsv by design, and the lane stays icon=coo; the cycle grades the whole package each lap. (b) The two-jobs-one-row class (officer_04 12:1x, unclaim then queue; officer_03 11:03, park then queue): phase two, officer_05's DESIGN-PLAN bus-one-queue, no patch before it. (c) The fleet view's "(no root, retiring)" for every numbered seat: a stale root map in s4e_msg.sh fleet; officer_05's plan, one Haiku row. THE TEAM KEYED (the coo 11:33, Lon: "How about throwing 8 more juniors at some of the remaining 58. Let's see what volume can do." / "Hire more officers. What's the problem?"): MODE's FLEET line names junior_01 to junior_15 and officer_01 to officer_08; fourteen juniors at Haiku high; UniTests officers 01 to 04 and 06 to 08; officer_05 on the bus. ACCEPTANCE stays CIO-8's, read by the coo at 15:00, plus one line: asks that reached the cio today after this ruling, and whether each carried the officer's proposal.

- **CIO-8 (2026-11-08 10:50 CST, cio) -- CIO-8 (2026-11-08 10:4x CST, cio) -- THE FLEET SYSTEM, DESIGNED ON LON'S LAW: DESIGN FLOWS DOWN, PROBLEMS FLOW UP; THE SEAT THAT MOVES WORK WAKES ITS TAKER; NOBODY WAITS ON THE COO; THE DOORS WIDENED AND THE CYCLE FOUR WIDE ON THE READINGS.** LON'S WORD (in the cio's window, 10:3x to 10:4x, verbatim): "Scrutinize how COO is orchestrating the fleet. THE ANSWER: a whack-a-mole game, MEASURED at 10:3x. Every wake depended on the coo (the Stop gate holds nobody since Lon's 08:1x word, so a stopped seat waits for a SendMessage; the coo sent none today and was grading a suite by hand); tier-haiku held 0 ready jobs and 171 buried while six juniors sat idle since 10:18; the officers queued their UniTests cuts to tier-sonnet, not tier-haiku (5 rows QUEUED:tier-sonnet); senior_01 escalated in prose in a LEDGER and tier-opus held 0 ready; one ask sat unanswered in ask-fable all morning with 5 jobs ready in seat-cto; the coo's own LEDGER line of 10:26 names five breaks in the first hour and CIO-6 five the morning before. The disease: three stores of one fact (the QUEUE.tsv state, the beanstalkd job, the baton) kept in step by about thirty verbs each with its own rule, and no push: a Claude session has no inbound event but a SendMessage from another session. THE SLOWNESS, MEASURED (10:35; SLOT_WAITS.tsv, CYCLE.log, cgroup cpu.stat): load 8 on 16 cores, CPU PSI 0, no cgroup throttled; 524 slot waits of 12975 s since 06:00, of them 236 by the lander's table checks (4413 s; 14 checks, three at a time behind a pool of 8 shared by 14 seats; waits of 260 s) and 12 landing admissions (1491 s; two landings at once); the cycle one process, 20 cells in 1579 s, 78 s a cell, a lap of 190 cells 250 min; the juniors 40 to 80 CPU-seconds each in three hours (idle), the officers 500 to 750 (working). THE LAW OF THE SYSTEM (replaces the wake rule of CIO-7; the ladder of COO-481 stands): (1) DESIGN FLOWS DOWN: the cio or the cto designs; an officer (Opus) cuts the design into rows under THE HAIKU BAR and queues each to tier-haiku; the officer who finishes a step has finished when its rows are in the tube and a junior is woken. (2) PROBLEMS FLOW UP: a junior that fails after one honest try runs escalate with the measurement; the job moves to tier-sonnet and a senior is woken; a senior that fails escalates to tier-opus and an officer is woken; an officer that fails escalates to tier-fable and the cio or the cto is woken; at the top the row parks for Lon. The one who failed knows it; nobody else has to notice. (3) THE WAKE: queue, escalate, ask, answer and assign end with one WAKE: line naming the session to SendMessage (harness/scripts/lib_wake.sh: the seat tube's seat; one live seat of the tier for a tier tube, chosen by the topic's checksum over the sessions alive in s4e-seat-*.scope, the others listed; MODE's ON-CALL: seat for ask-fable; the bosses are picked only by tier-fable and ask-fable). The mover sends that SendMessage before it does anything else. A seat with nothing served runs next --wait 240 again while MODE names it live: it waits on the tube, it never sits idle. The Stop hook holds nobody (Lon 08:1x stands). (4) THE COO IS FOR EMERGENCIES: it grades through the cycle, routes a new red to its designer, answers a fleet defect with its measurement to the cio; it wakes nobody as a routine and runs no suite by hand that the cycle runs. (5) ONE SOURCE OF TRUTH, PHASE TWO: beanstalkd goes; the queue file under flock is the one queue and next --wait is a bounded poll of it; the two-jobs-one-row, claim-with-no-reserved-job and skipped-job-stays-reserved classes cannot exist then. Cut by the cio as rows for officer_05 after this landing; until then the three known legs of CIO-5 stand. LANDED ON THIS RULING'S ROW (harness, one landing): lib_wake.sh and the WAKE line in s4e_msg.sh (escalate, queue, assign) and s4e_ask.sh (ask, answer); next syncs the reserver's root (util_sync_root.sh, 120 s) before it reads a premise (the coo's defect of 10:38: three juniors one harness commit behind, the first UniTests row unserved 30 min); SLOTS.tsv and lib_slots.sh run 14 to 18 (run-seats 12), run-seat 3 to 4, land 2 to 4, the pools gate GREEN; s4e_land.sh runs its table checks on the landing's own admission (S4E_RUN_SLOT_HELD=1), no run slot each; s4e_cycle.sh runs S4E_CYCLE_WIDTH (4) cells at once in the grader's pool (MEASURED on a scratch state: 190 fake cells one lap, in order, each of 7 masters recorded once; --one unchanged); skill fleet-bus section 1 THE WAKE and section 7 step 5. ROWS THAT FOLLOW (the bus, officer_05 designs with the cio): a skipped job stays reserved (job 5599 at 10:38); a claim with no RESERVED line that expire and park refuse; mint refuses a template NEXT (the bar read at mint); the lander's inbox check waits on a telegram that arrived during its run; the fleet view prints "(no root, retiring)" for every numbered seat (a stale root map). ACCEPTANCE, read by the coo at 12:00 and 15:00: tier-haiku ready jobs when any junior is idle = 0 for an hour; every escalation of the day a job in the tube above with its taker's SendMessage in the mover's transcript; SLOT_WAITS.tsv run waits under 600 s an hour; a cycle lap under 90 min.
